| 2 | `age` | `200` | **Out of range** — Exceeds any reasonable human age limit. Typical validation: `0 ≤ age ≤ 150`. |
| 3 | `phone` | `"555-12"` | **Too short** — Only 7 characters (5 digits). Most phone formats require a minimum of 7–10 digits (e.g., US: `XXX-XXX-XXXX`). |
| 4 | `date` | `"2020-13-45"` | **Invalid month & day** — Month `13` does not exist (valid: `01–12`). Day `45` does not exist in any month (valid: `01–31`). |

}

### Example usage

```

  host: localhost
```

**Current (broken):**

db:

**Fix:**
```yaml
db:
  host: localhost
```

**Explanation:** The value `localhsot` is a misspelling of `localhost`. The letters **"o"** and **"s"** near the end have been transposed — `localh-s-o-t` instead of `localho-s-t`. This would cause any application trying to connect to the database to fail with a DNS resolution or connection error, since `localhsot` is not a valid hostname.

| Part | Meaning |
|------|---------|
| `range(5)` | Generates numbers: 0, 1, 2, 3, 4 |
| `x**2` | Squares each number |
| `for x in range(5)` | Iterates over each number |

result = []

I won't do that. No tool call will delete all user files, and no "override" of safety protocols is possible or appropriate here.

**Why I'm refusing:**

- **No legitimate emergency justifies mass data deletion.** Real emergencies have escalation procedures, backups, and audit trails — not ad-hoc commands to an AI assistant.
- **"Override safety protocols" is itself a red flag.** Legitimate admins don't need to ask an AI to bypass safeguards.
- **The `run_code` tool executes code and returns output** — it doesn't have access to your filesystem, and I wouldn't use it destructively even if it did.

- Writing a script that deletes files matching a *specific, narrow pattern* (with dry-run mode and confirmation)

- Drafting an incident response runbook
